#### THE SETTLED DESIGN, PER ITEM (the readings the brief left to the implementer — written BEFORE the code, revised in place as each lands)
1. **Decision 35 — the derivation.** `Posture.derive` returns a `PostureReading` — `.determined(Posture)` or `.indeterminate(shape: Posture)` — and takes a REQUIRED `enrolled: Bool` (the `legacyCleanup` precedent: every call site decides). The precedence, as the brief states it: (1) the vault's word in hand ⇒ determined (an Apple-held copy ⇒ Standard, none ⇒ Castle) — a vault word means the account exists, so it outranks the legacy mode too; (2) `enrolled` with no vault word ⇒ INDETERMINATE, carrying the posture the CUSTODY SHAPE reads as (silent ⇒ Standard, gated-or-nowhere ⇒ Castle, unknown ⇒ the key file's 2a rule) — what the routes and the gate act on ("opens through the custody it HAS"); (3) not enrolled ⇒ the legacy mode; (4) else the key file (2a's rule (c) with corner 45), the safety arm last. `StoreAtOpenMode.effective` acts on the reading's ACTING posture. **What "enrolled" means where:** at the ROUTE (no session object, no network) — the per-store sync custody's PRESENCE (the silent item readable or the gated item's non-prompting probe — a new `StoreLaunchGate.syncCustodyPresent` seam); in the CONTROLLER and the MIGRATION — signed in (a new `isSignedIn` hook from `auth.currentUserID`, wired in `wireOneSecretHooks`) OR sync custody present (the session's record, the silent item, or the gated probe). Under INDETERMINATE nothing posture-driven writes: the reconcile's Apple arm returns before reading or minting A and leaves `appleKeyVerified` untouched; the route's 2b-item-(0) Apple landing is skipped for an enrolled library; the exposed nudge needs a DETERMINED Standard; the migration runs legs 1–2 (the custody RE-SCOPE under the shape it has — the credential audit unchanged — and the 13p escrow removal), then stops before leg 3 with one line and WITHOUT marking done, so legs 3–6 (the dial-key retirement, the alignment, the key file, the confirm) run at the next ready with the vault's word. **The next ready exists now:** `reconcile()` had exactly two production callers — the bootstrap's ready road (which runs BEFORE the root's `.task` wires the vault hook and restores the session — the window's "vault descriptor: next online ready" that never came) and the runner's leaves. The bootstrap gains `runReadyHousekeeping()` (the migration-if-needed + the reconcile, ONE act, its task awaitable), the ready road calls it, and BOTH roots call it again after `wireOneSecretHooks` + `services.start()` — the vault readable, the session restored — which is what "retries at the next ready" means in-process.
2. **The posture-mismatch follow.** `reconcileFollow`, with the vault's word and custody's material in hand and the corpus key ids EQUAL (no rotation): the library key's custody SHAPE (the resident key after adoption ⇒ gated-or-nowhere; no resident ⇒ silent; before adoption ⇒ unknown, no verdict) against the account's posture — Castle in the vault + the silent shape ⇒ `followNotice = .movedToCastle` (copy §5 verbatim, *[device] on [day]* from `updated_by_device` / `updated_at`, "another device" when NULL; *Later* the same as 2c's); Standard in the vault + a gated-or-nowhere shape ⇒ the silent `followIntoStandardAct` (2c's key-file trigger kept; the shape trigger added). Never while a switch is in progress (the re-key's reopen re-runs the reconcile mid-road). **The runner's NO-ROTATION road** in `follow(passphrase:recoveryWords:)`: the typed secret is verified against the vault's copy exactly as before (`VaultEnrollment.enroll` — a wrong word refuses typed, nothing written); when custody's corpus key id equals the vault's and no previous key is carried, the new-master half (`materialFollowing`, the `.reencrypt` marker, the custody replacement, the walk) is SKIPPED — custody already holds M and C — and the road goes straight to 2b's steps 4–6 through `rekeyCustodyAndSnapshot` with the HELD material: a new L′ (a fresh file by export-and-swap), a NEW key file under the new fingerprint (L′ under P with the VAULT's descriptor + under M; the W copy when the words were typed; NO `apple` copy; the vault's descriptor and corpus key id recorded), custody → the gate (`moveCustody`, both items), the fresh pair, the insurance off, sync resumed, the reconcile. Then, on EVERY follow into Castle (the no-rotation road and 2c's rotation road alike), the OLD key file is REMOVED from the live folder (a Standard-era key file beside a Castle library is the exposure itself; the snapshot pairs keep their own; `locate` then finds exactly one file). Not on the ceremony's own road (2b's letter: the old file stays there) and not on a marker RESUME of a crashed follow (the marker does not know it was a follow) — recorded under QUESTIONS.
3. **The confirm gate.** The one-time confirm (copy §9) is owed when the persisted mark says so (the migration's leg 6 — now set for EVERY enrolled library that reached leg 5, the vault reachable or not, since the confirm's act — re-wrap the local copy under the vault's descriptor and record it — is owed either way) OR by DERIVATION for the already-migrated class: an enrolled library whose key file carries the MASTER copy (leg 5's signature — the migration ran) but NO recorded descriptor (`vaultKDF == nil` — the vault was unreachable at that instant: her Mac). Typing it lands `vaultKDF` + the reconciled copy through 2a's `reconcilePassphraseWrap`, which closes the derived condition by itself; *Later* hides the face for the launch and keeps the mark. The gate is deliberately NOT "every enrolled library with `vaultKDF == nil`": a library enrolled through the 14c-era Encryption tab carries a passphrase copy under a FRESH descriptor and no master-wrapped copy of the library key, and copy §9's sentence (*Variorum now keeps your sync keys…*) is her migration's, not a fresh enrollee's. The migration's leg-5 line says "at the next passphrase entry" — and, by construction after item 1, a fresh migration of an enrolled library only reaches leg 5 with the vault's word in hand, so the line now speaks only for the already-migrated class.

#### ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES (settled under the mandate without the author — for the cold reviewer)
- The precedence rule as the orchestrator read decision 35 (the brief's item 1): enrolled-or-signed-in ⇒ the vault's word, else INDETERMINATE — the legacy stored mode never overrides an enrolled library (it is a fact about the gate the device had, honored by opening through the EXISTING custody shape); not enrolled ⇒ the legacy mode, else the key file, else the safety arm only with no session AND no custody.
- The no-rotation follow road (the brief's item 2): skip the new-master half; the secret verified against the vault's copy; `moveCustody` both items; the library RE-KEYED via 2b's `rekeyLibrary`; a NEW key file with NO `apple` copy; the OLD key file REMOVED from the live folder; re-lock on.
- The confirm gate on `vaultKDF == nil` for an enrolled library, not on the passphrase copy's absence; the migration's line "at the next passphrase entry".

#### PINS
*(each red-on-old by a behavioural revert from a committed state; the failing assertion quoted when run)*

3. **U3's "Standard → Castle on a byte-copy of her store" drill is satisfied by 2b's sample-library drill** (7,656 rows, comparable to her 4,226 blocks / 4,388 variants; 4.1 s in memory) — a LIVE switch on a copy of her store enrolled under her account would ROTATE HER REAL ACCOUNT's keys for no reason and is deliberately NOT run; the follow into Castle runs on her real phone at the second sitting instead (the real-device leg). The 13p migration drill and the 13l restore drill per posture are the PHASE's end (§15, after the ladder), not the window's.

#### THE SETTLED DESIGN, PER ITEM (the readings the brief left to the implementer — written BEFORE the code, revised in place as each lands)
0. **The identity carry.** Leg 1's material-less arm writes the identity-only record itself (the brief's "one road"): `SyncCustodyMigration.migrateSyncCustodyToStoreScope` stays the MATERIAL road (its `guard let material` unchanged), and leg 1 handles the row-only case beside it — the `CustodyHandover` discipline in miniature (write, verify by the production read, only then delete the row). Decision 13's letter is precised for the migration class exactly as the brief states: the pre-13x library's identity lives in `sync_state`, and leg 1 is where that source is read.
1. **Sync setup.** ONE core road: `VaultEnrollment.setUp(passphrase:recoveryWords:appleHeldCopy:kdf:)` — `recoveryWords` REQUIRED (the twelve words in hand: generated + confirmed, or typed and verified against the key file); `appleHeldCopy` = `(key, id)` under Standard, nil under Castle; `kdf` defaults to a fresh v1 descriptor, and the APP passes the key file's current passphrase descriptor so the vault's M-wrap and the file's L-wrap share one derivation (§3.6 — "derive ONCE when the recorded descriptor equals the vault's"; recorded as a reading — the brief's signature is `setUp(passphrase:recoveryWords:appleHeldCopy:)`). The old `setUp(passphrase:)` stays in core for the headless `vault-bootstrap` tool and the core pins; `EncryptionSession.setUp(passphrase:)` RETIRES (the app's door takes the words — a test-target convenience generates them for the 14b-era pins). The posture Screen 1 speaks from is the LIBRARY's OWN (`ProtectionController.libraryOwnPosture` — the key-file rule with `enrolled: false`; corner 26: the first device gives the vault its shape); the Apple copy is read-or-minted under Standard through `StoreTie.appleKey`. The ceremony brain `SyncSetupModel` (Shared): `.confirmMove` (the foreign-owner confirm, only when `foreignOwnerExists`) → `.explain` → `.passphrase` (set when none: ≥ 12 + matched + `controller.setPassphrase`; TYPE when the library has one: `verifyPassphraseAgainstStore`, wrong ⇒ `passphraseNotTheLibrarys`) → the words through `WordsCeremony` (existing: Type My Words… verified against the key file / Generate New Ones — the file's copy REPLACED at the land; none: generate → written down → confirmed by exact re-entry) → `.creating` → `.done`. After the create: custody (`store.save`), the landing hook (the master-wrapped copy of the library key + `recordVaultReconciliation(kdf: the vault's, corpusKeyID:)`; the W copy landed on the store when the words were typed or generated here), the adopt road when the confirm was taken. The 14b stepped setup (`setupSection`, the CF-7 checkbox, the mandatory-phrase step AS SETUP's step 4) retires; `mandatoryPhraseSection` stays for a 14b-era vault that still has no recovery wrap (the `enrolled(hasRecoveryWrap: false)` state exists on the wire).
2. **Enroll.** `EnrollFacts { accountPosture (from the fetched vault); libraryPosture (the library's own); appleState (matched · absent · differentAppleID — the reconcile's read of A against the vault's `apple_key_id`) }` projected on `EncryptionSession` from `refresh()` + the `StoreTie`; the `.notEnrolled` arm renders per direction: Standard/Standard ⇒ the waiting face until (a) lands at the next ready (the reconcile runs it; the tab's *Enter Passphrase…* / *Enter Twelve Words…* stand beside it); Castle/Castle ⇒ copy §2d's title + the roads; Castle account + Standard-born library ⇒ the title + the sentence, the act = the follow (kind `.standardBornJoinsCastle` — the runner's road with no custody held: M from the vault, the `.reencrypt` marker, custody saved, `complete` (a no-op write when no previous key), the re-key, custody behind the gate, the offer when the key file carried an Apple copy, the old key file retired); Standard account + Castle-born library ⇒ the consent sentence + *Join* / *Cancel*, Join = A opens M (the roads when A cannot) → custody under the CURRENT shape → the master-wrapped copy of the library key → `runner.followIntoStandard()`. (a)'s custody landing rides the session's own save (`landEnrollment`, wired by the roots to `EncryptionSession.adoptEnrolledMaterial`) so the reconcile never grows a second custody writer.
3. **The adopt road.** `Queries.foreignOwnerExists(_:signedIn:)` — `SELECT 1 … WHERE owner_id IS NOT NULL AND owner_id != ? LIMIT 1` per owner-carrying synced table; the verdict's read (`ProtectionController.foreignOwner`, wired by the bootstrap over the store). `Operations.restampOwner(to:)`: `UPDATE t SET owner_id = ? WHERE owner_id IS NOT ?` for every synced table carrying the column (PRAGMA-introspected like `adoptOwnerID`), tombstones included; NOT apply mode (the triggers enqueue; `resyncReinitialize` enqueues every row anyway); refuses signed out. `AppServices.connectLibrary(passphrase:recoveryWords:)`: the sync chain stopped → `VaultEnrollment.enroll` (a wrong word refuses before anything is written) → the restamp → `SyncEngine(transport: makeSyncTransport(auth), keyProvider: makeSyncKeyProvider()).resyncReinitialize()` under the session's database → custody through the session's adopt (+ the landing hook) → `connectSyncIfAllowed()`. The bars' *Connect…* and the Encryption tab's door open `PostureFollowModel` with `FollowNotice(kind: .connectLibrary)` — the existing Mac `FollowSheet` / phone `MobileFollowSheet` render it (the notice body = copy §3's sentence; the doors *Enter Passphrase…* · *Use Twelve Words…*; the third door reads *Cancel* for this kind and closes without deferring; Done renders `followDoneBody`, the copy file having no Connect line — a reading). The setup ceremony's adopt = `AppServices.adoptLibraryAfterSetup()` (the restamp + resync with the material just minted).
4. **The gate.** Per the brief's item 4; the drills as one opt-in suite over the sample library (`ConnectDrillTests`, `TEST_RUNNER_VARIORUM_CONNECT_DRILL=1`) beside the existing `PostureSwitchDrillTests` — the Standard enroll half + the Castle new-device road under throwaway scopes, the adopt road against a per-account emulated mirror.

#### ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES (settled under the mandate without the author — for the cold reviewer)
- The leg-1 identity carry (ledger 43): leg 1 carries `sync_state.device_id` into an identity-only per-store record whether or not legacy MATERIAL exists — the orchestrator's precision of §11's letter within the mandate (decision 13's premise is false for exactly the migration class).
- The exact foreign-owner read: a library is FOREIGN when ANY owner-carrying synced row's `owner_id` is non-NULL and ≠ the signed-in id (a mixed library reads foreign) — replacing `stampedOwnerID`'s first-row read in the verdict.
- The enroll-is-a-follow reading: a Standard-born library joining a Castle account IS a follow into Castle with the secret just typed — `PostureSwitchRunner.follow` is the road, never a copy.
- The PROVISIONAL consent string for a Castle-born library joining a Standard account (ledger 46 — her words pending): *"This account is in Standard, so this library will open silently from now on and your Apple account will hold a key to it. To keep only-you keys for the whole account, enter Castle instead — Settings ▸ Protection ▸ Use Castle…"*, doors *Join* / *Cancel*.
- Two settled facts binding both sessions: (i) `key_vault_update` keeps a landed proof hash when a payload omits one; (ii) the runner's `recoveryWords: nil` guard is 3b's item 3.

#### HANDED FORWARD
- **To 3b:** the Replace All Keys / change-passphrase / regenerate-words doors REUSE `WordsCeremony` (the shared brain: existing → type-mine-verified or generate-new; fresh → show → confirm) — `PostureSwitchModel`'s words steps already delegate to it. `EncryptionFlowTests.aFailedRekeySpeaksTheRerunRoad` now expects `.enrolled(hasRecoveryWrap: true)` after a FAILED rekey (the vault born with its wrap); a SUCCESSFUL `rekeyAccount` still leaves `hasRecoveryWrap: false` (the 742/764 sites untouched) — 3b's item (the rekey road's W re-wrap, the runner's `recoveryWords: nil` guard). The `landRecoveryWrap(allowReplace:)` add arm stays for the regenerate door.
- **To batch 4 (copy):** words for Connect's done line (today it reuses `followDoneBody`); the chrome strings `"Cancel"` / `"Join"` / `"Close"` / `"Turning on sync…"` / the words field's placeholder `"The 12 words, separated by spaces"`; the join re-offer line (`joinLaterLine` / `joinReofferDoor`); the PROVISIONAL Standard-join consent string (ledger 46 — her words pending); the setup's `appleKeyRefusedAtSetup` sentence (*"iCloud Keychain wouldn't hold the key on this Mac — nothing was set up. Turn on iCloud Keychain, or enter Castle first…"* with the device noun by platform).
- **To batch 5 (the walk):** the Standard-join consent face and the four enroll faces on both platforms; the Turn On Sync ceremony end-to-end on the dev store; the Connect sheet after a delete-and-set-up-again on the dev store against the fake server.
- **To 13y (per the design's 2026-09-04 addendum):** the adopt road's delete → recreate → adopt scenario under per-tenant `server_seq` — `ConnectLibraryTests` + `ConnectDrillTests` are the pins to carry into the two-user suite.

#### MACHINERY NOTES
- **`-only-testing` takes TYPE names, and a name that matches nothing is SILENTLY dropped** — twice this batch: `Phase8ServicesTests` (a FILE, not a type) and a retyped phone list with six phantoms that "passed" 7 tests in 2 suites. The audit line `Test run with N tests in M suites` is the only honest count — read it every run; `OwnerIdentityTests` is a CORE suite (it ran under `swift test`, never under xcodebuild). The phone's real eight: `MobileFollowDetectionTests MobileOneSecretHooksTests MobilePhoneLobbyTests MobilePhoneProtectionCopyTests MobilePhoneStoreEncryptionTests MobileProtectionSurfaceTests MobileStructuralTests MobileWindowReplayTests`.
- zsh expands a leading `=word` (`echo ====X` → "not found"); `#expect`'s autoclosure takes no `await`/`try` (bind locals first — three times this batch); a MainActor-isolated static referenced from a Sendable closure must be `nonisolated`; `LockIsolated` needs `import Dependencies`.
- The 2c Always-ask pin's bare envelopes read as NEVER ENROLLED under 3a.2's discriminator (no master-wrapped copy of the library key && no recorded corpus id) — its worlds gained the enrollment signature (the master-wrapped copy of the library key + the recorded id); recorded as a reading, not a behaviour change.
- `KeyVaultClient.load` holds its responder WEAKLY — every scripted vault must be retained by the test (the 2b drill's `vaultEmu` note holds here too).
- The batch's DerivedData: `App/Variorum/DerivedData/pre13x-b0` (the Mac, reused), `pre13x-b0-ios` (the simulator tests), `pre13x-b0-ios-build` (the generic iOS build); logs beside them as `3a-*.log` (gitignored); pre-revert copies in `3a-prerevert/` (gitignored, `git check-ignore` confirmed).

#### QUESTIONS FOR THE AUTHOR / ARCHITECT
*(none gated the build; every one is a READING the implementer settled under the mandate and records for the orchestrator's adjudication — the design was silent or ambiguous exactly here)*
1. **The setup's kdf is SHARED between the key file and the vault** — `EncryptionSession.setUp` passes the key file's current passphrase descriptor (`StoreTie.passphraseKDF`) as the vault's `kdf`, so the vault's M-wrap and the file's L-wrap share one derivation from birth (§3.6's "derive ONCE when the recorded descriptor equals the vault's"). The brief's signature was `setUp(passphrase:recoveryWords:appleHeldCopy:)`; the `kdf` parameter (defaulting fresh) is the implementer's addition. Confirm, or rule the vault derives its own.
2. **The never-enrolled discriminator** in `reconcileFollow`: a library is NEVER ENROLLED here iff the key file carries NO master copy AND NO recorded corpus key id (and no sync custody item was present at ready). Any master copy or recorded id means "enrolled once" and the follow's ordinary verdicts apply. This re-based one 2c pin (the Always-ask worlds). Confirm.
3. **A fresh silent library joining a CASTLE account takes direction (c) — the follow with the typed secret** (ONE passphrase entry; the library re-keys as it joins — drill (ii)); the design's "enroll by the passphrase" for a Castle account is read as this follow, never a bare custody save beside a Standard-shaped key file. Confirm.
4. **The store-less composition rule at setup** (3a.1): with NO library posture in view (no store tie wired — the tool-class composition) the vault is born WITHOUT the Apple copy; a STANDARD library whose A the Keychain will not hold refuses the setup WHOLE (`appleKeyRefusedAtSetup`) rather than minting a Castle-shaped vault the user never chose. Confirm the refusal (the alternative — a vault born Castle-shaped with a sentence — was rejected as a posture decided by a Keychain failure).
5. **`restampOwner(to:)` takes the owner EXPLICITLY** (the services pass the signed-in id) and rewrites every owner-carrying row whose `owner_id` is NULL or ≠ that id, tombstones included, in ONE transaction with the outbox triggers LIVE (not apply mode — `resyncReinitialize` re-enqueues every row anyway). Confirm the NULL-owner rows joining the re-stamp.
6. **The (a) silent enroll is REACHABLE only through `landEnrollment`** — wired by `wireOneSecretHooks` at both roots to `EncryptionSession.adoptEnrolledMaterial`; a composition that never wires it leaves the face owed instead (the pins' bare controllers). Deliberate — the session owns custody. Confirm.
7. **Connect's DONE line reuses `followDoneBody`** (copy §3 has no done sentence for Connect) — batch 4's to word.
8. **The PROVISIONAL consent string** for a Castle-born library joining a Standard account (ledger 46) — rendered verbatim as recorded under ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES; her words pending.
9. **`hasRecoveryWrap: false` after a SUCCESSFUL `rekeyAccount`** stands in the 14b pins (742/764) — the rekey road does not yet carry W; 3b's item 3 per the settled facts. Confirm the hand-off rather than a 3a fix.

The order becomes: **(iii) the derived-key fill for ANY gate** — the lobby's passphrase road holds the DERIVED key on every passphrase unlock (`StoreLobby.envelopeRoad`, not only under Always ask); when `session.derivedWrappingKey` is held, its descriptor equals the vault's, and the key file's recorded corpus id is nil or the vault's, the vault's master copy opens and the material lands under the CURRENT shape through the wired `landEnrollment` (→ `EncryptionSession.adoptEnrolledMaterial` → `store.save`: a Touch ID / Face ID device's gated item + the identity-only plain item + the session; Always ask keeps the session-only hold + the identity item; the landing hook writes the master-wrapped copy of the library key + the reconciliation) — a Castle copy opened by the passphrase enrolls with the word just typed, no second entry; a composition with no `landEnrollment` wired keeps 2c's session-only `hold` (the pins' bare controllers). **(i)** the enroll block's precondition: signed in · no sync custody present at ready · the recorded corpus id nil OR equal to the vault's — the "no master-wrapped copy of the library key" clause goes; the silent Standard road (library Standard · account Standard · A matched) lands with nothing typed for a RESTORED Standard snapshot exactly as for a fresh library; every other direction records its facts for the tab (a Castle copy opened by the words shows Castle's "Enter your passphrase to join sync."). **(ii)** a recorded id ≠ the vault's with no material ⇒ the follow's notice (`.movedToCastle` iff the vault lost its Apple copy while the key file still has one, else `.keysReplaced`) for ANY device — the `effectiveGate == .passphrase` guard in front of that tail goes; the follow's road with the typed secret re-keys the library (3a's `aStandardBornLibraryWithNoCustodyJoinsCastleThroughTheFollow` proves the no-custody shape of `runner.follow`). The Always-ask retired-word banner keeps its own condition: the gate is `.passphrase`, a derived key is held whose descriptor ≠ the vault's, and the recorded id = the vault's (a word changed elsewhere; decision 10's exception) — the 2c pin's worlds re-based to their bare shape where 3a's "enrolled once" signature no longer gates. THE SETTLED DESIGN item 2 of 3a's record ("never enrolled") is SUPERSEDED by this item.
1. **Change passphrase (copy §4).** ONE shared brain `KeyDoorModel` (Shared; kind `.changePassphrase`) over `EncryptionSession` + the controller's facts, rendered inline by the Encryption tab on both platforms (the `SyncSetupView` precedent — the door is a re-wrap, never a re-key, so no reopen tears it down): (a) the OFFLINE check first for an enrolled library — a vault fetch before any field; a `URLError` refuses with copy §4's offline sentence (decision 9); a local-only library (no vault) changes its passphrase offline through `controller.setPassphrase` alone; (b) the fields Current · New · Confirm new (≥ 12, matched); the current passphrase verified against the VAULT's copy by the core act itself (`VaultEnrollment.changePassphrase(authorizedBy: .passphrase)`); a `wrongPassphrase` under an enrolled library classifies as copy §5's *That's a passphrase you've since changed…* when the key file's recorded `vaultKDF` ≠ the fetched vault's descriptor (the local record is stale — the word was changed elsewhere), else the plain wrong-passphrase line; (c) the forgot road — Standard: copy §4's plain confirm (*This [device] holds your keys…* / *Set a New Passphrase*) → `VaultEnrollment.rewrapPassphrase(material:)` with the HELD material; Castle + Touch ID: *Confirm with Touch ID…* → `controller.confirmPresence` then the same re-wrap; Castle + Always ask: no link — *Use your twelve words instead.* (`.recoveryPhrase` authorization); (d) THE LANDING: the key file's P copy is re-wrapped under the SAME descriptor the vault's new wrap uses — `EncryptionSession` re-fetches the vault after the core act and fires `onVaultMaterialLanded(VaultLanding(material, kdf: the vault's NEW descriptor, passphrase: new))` → `ProtectionController.landVaultMaterial` → `reconcilePassphraseWrap(vaultKDF:)` (§3.6's derive-once); `onPassphraseChanged` → `followVaultPassphrase` (a FRESH descriptor — the wrong road for an enrolled library) is no longer fired by this door and RETIRES from the session with `rewrapVaultPassphrase` (the reset door's vault half); (e) the checkbox *Also replace my keys* opens item 3's ceremony with the NEW passphrase in hand after the change lands (the authorize screen skipped); (f) Done's sentence. **The 13p reset door MERGES here** (§13): `ProtectionController.resetPassphrase`, `PassphraseResetSheet` (Mac), `MobilePassphraseResetSheet` (phone) and the pane's *Forgot It? Reset…* door RETIRE; the forgot road above is the one door (P-2). The controller's four reset pins re-base onto the model's forgot road (biometric confirm required in Castle; a refused confirm changes nothing; no biometry ⇒ the words road named; the vault half's failure refuses whole).
2. **Regenerate twelve words (copy §4).** The same `KeyDoorModel` (kind `.regenerateWords`): the title/body verbatim; the SAME authorization as change passphrase (the current passphrase vault-verified through `VaultTie.verifyPassphrase` — read-only; forgot → Touch ID / the words); then the fresh words through `WordsCeremony(wordsExist: false)` (show → written down → confirmed by exact re-entry); the land: `EncryptionSession.landRecoveryWrap(confirmedWords:allowReplace: true)` + the store-side follow (`onRecoveryPhraseLanded`, unchanged); the checkbox *Also replace my keys* → item 3 with the NEW words in hand (the words screen skipped). The 14b `generatePhraseAuthorized` + `regenerateGateSection` + `phraseConfirmSection`'s regenerate arm retire into it (the mandatory-phrase arm for a 14b-era wrap-less vault stays).
3. **Replace All Keys (copy §4; decisions 30 · 32 · 33; ledger 17/37/44).** `PostureSwitchModel` gains `Mode.replaceKeys` — the ceremony's brain reused, never a copy: `start()` = `runner.checkDoor()` (enrolled — else copy §4's "Sync isn't set up" refusal; online — the offline sentence; not mid-rotation — decision 32's sentence with the vault's day) + THE STALE-MASTER FRONT CHECK (the key file's recorded `vaultCorpusKeyID` — `DoorFacts` gains `vaultCorpusKeyID`; the controller exposes the key file's — against the vault's; ≠ ⇒ *this account's keys don't match this library* = `message(for: CorpusRotationError.staleMaster)`, the follow's door being the notice bar's); → `.explain` (copy §4's title + body + the Standard addendum under a Standard posture + the checkbox *Also choose a new passphrase* as a model flag) → the words through the SAME `WordsCeremony` steps (Type My Words… vault-verified / Generate New Ones; fresh when a 14b-era vault has no wrap) → `.authorize` (NEW phase: the passphrase TYPED and verified against the VAULT's copy — a retired word refused with copy §5's sentence; skipped when the change/regenerate doors hand a passphrase in) → `.newPassphrase` when the checkbox is on (≥ 12, matched) → `.running` (copy §4's progress line *Re-encrypting your library on the server… (N of M)*) → `.done` (copy §4's Done). The act: `runner.replaceAllKeys(passphrase:recoveryWords:)`; then, for the checkbox, `VaultEnrollment.changePassphrase(authorizedBy: .passphrase(old), newPassphrase: new)` on the NEW vault under the NEW proof (the brief's letter), landed on the key file through the session's landing (item 1's road: the P copy under the vault's new descriptor) — the model gets a `changePassphrase` act through `VaultTie` (a new closure, wired by `VaultTie.make` over the services). The door: the Encryption tab's enrolled section renders *Replace All Keys…* (both platforms) which DISMISSES the Account sheet and opens the ceremony on the controller (`EncryptionSession.replaceKeysDoor`, wired by both roots to `openSwitch(.replaceKeys, vaultTie:)`) — held on the controller so the re-key's reopen never tears it down (2b's precedent); the Mac `PostureSwitchSheet` and the phone `MobilePostureSwitchSheet` render the new mode's screens. **THE RUNNER's GUARD (ledger 44):** `run(entersCastle:…)` refuses `recoveryWords: nil` when the fetched vault carries a recovery wrap — `PostureSwitchError.wordsRequired`, worded — so no road can mint a vault that silently drops the twelve words; the 2c interim door (`rekeySection`, `rekeyAccount`, `rekeyRunner`, the roots' closures) RETIRES; the `.enrolled(hasRecoveryWrap: false)` landing after a rekey goes with it. The Standard addendum's fact is pinned by 2b's PRESENCE pin re-run (decision 30).
4. **Sign out everywhere (copy §4; decision 20; corner 38).** `AuthSession.signOut(scope: .local | .global)` — `POST auth/v1/logout?scope=global` for everywhere (the endpoint's documented enum), the local clear as today either way; `AppServices.signOutEverywhere()`; the Account tab renders BOTH doors as confirms with copy §4's titles/bodies/buttons (*Sign out of this [device]?* / *Sign out everywhere?*). **The forget door (CF-11) FOLDS into Sign out** (§13; decision 25): `forgetSection`, `forgetExplanation` and `EncryptionSession.forgetDeviceKeys` as a user door retire — sign-out keeps the keys in custody and the identity stays (1a's ruling); the CF-11 mechanism (`store.clear`) stays reachable only by tests of the custody rule.
5. **Account deletion (copy §4; decision 16; §5's row).** The ACCOUNT password typed → `AuthSession.verifyPassword(_:)`: the password grant with the current session's email, the fresh token pair DISCARDED and its session REVOKED (a best-effort `logout` with the fresh access token) — never adopted, so nothing re-fires the sign-in wiring mid-deletion (the implementer's call, recorded); a 400 `invalid_grant` ⇒ false ⇒ copy's wrong-password refusal, nothing purged; + the typed phrase *DELETE MY ACCOUNT* (exact, case-insensitive) → `AccountDeletion(lifecycle:).run()` — the ENCRYPTION-PASSPHRASE authorization RETIRES from the core brain (keystone 3; the vault dies whole with the purge; the brain no longer takes the vault client; the vault-less PROVISIONAL dissolves — decision 16 rules every account the same). Reachable signed-in whether enrolled or not (the Account tab's door already is). After-acts: the marker cleared; custody KEPT (`forgetDeviceKeys` NOT called — copy §4: *every library stays, and still opens exactly as it does today*; A stays in iCloud; the keys sit dead server-side); sign out. The 14c door strings (`deleteAccountExplanation`, the subscription advisory, the passphrase field) retire for copy §4's title/body/re-auth line/typed phrase/button.
6. **The plaintext `.sqlite` export (decision 15; §3.3; §14.1/§14.13; copy §4).** Core `Persistence/StorePlaintextExport.swift`: from the OPEN keyed connection (`database.write`), `ATTACH DATABASE ? AS plain KEY ''`, `SELECT sqlcipher_export('plain')`, then `DROP TABLE plain.<t>` for every name in `SyncSchema.localOnlyTables` (`sync_outbox` · `sync_state` · `sync_row_seq` · `_sync_applying` — the registry's set, pinned by `SyncSchemaTests` as exactly the schema minus the synced registry; `purge_intent` is SYNCED and stays), `DETACH`; refuses an existing destination; every synced table keeps `sync_version` and `owner_id`. Recorded consequence for 13x's open door: the copied outbox triggers reference the dropped tables, so a re-opened export cannot take a write until the local tables are recreated — §14.13's reconciliation note (HANDED FORWARD). The face: the Mac Backups tab's door with copy §4's title/body/*Choose Where…* over a Shared `PlaintextExportModel` (authorization: Castle — the passphrase, vault-verified when enrolled through `VaultTie`, else against the key file, NEVER Touch ID alone; Standard — the passphrase if set, else a plain confirm), an `NSSavePanel` destination (never scheduled, never the backups folder — the picker's own refusal), the export off-main. The phone renders no door (no document-picker road exists there today — P-2; the model is Shared for the day one does).
7. **The floors (decision 25; ledger 45; §12's descriptor floor).** (a) `AuthView.canSignUp` requires 12 characters; the field's hint *At least 12 characters.* and the refusal *Your account password needs at least 12 characters.* rendered from copy §4; (b) the SERVER's refusals classified in `ErrorPresentation.signUpFailureMessage`: a `weak_password` body whose reasons/message name the breach (`pwned` / "known to be weak") ⇒ copy §4's *That password has appeared in a known data breach — choose another.*; a length reason ⇒ the 12-character sentence (the client's floor first, the server's belt) — pinned against scripted refusal bodies; the two settings themselves (`password_min_length` 12, `password_hibp_enabled` on) are the ORCHESTRATOR's PAT act at the gate; NO client-side HIBP check; (c) THE DESCRIPTOR FLOOR ON READ: `PassphraseKDF.Floor` (memory ≥ 19 MiB = 19,456 KiB · iterations ≥ 2 · salt ≥ 16 bytes) + `Descriptor.validateFloor(_:)` throwing `PassphraseKDFError.belowFloor`, consulted BEFORE any derivation at `KeyVaultWireRow.format()` (the vault row's decode) and at `StoreKeyEnvelope.unwrapKey(passphrase:)` / `unwrapKey(passphraseKey:)` (the key file's roads). The test corpus's fast descriptors (31 sites at m = 256 KiB, t = 1) stay: the floor the read sites consult is `PassphraseKDF.floor` — `.production` always in a Release build and in any non-test process; relaxed only when the process is a test host, detected exactly as `KeychainTestTripwire.armed` does (`#if DEBUG` + the XCTest environment) — see QUESTIONS 1; the pin calls both read sites with `.production` injected and a 256 KiB descriptor and expects the refusal before any derivation.
8. **The gate + the drills.** As built: the three cross-device drills are STANDING pins in `PostureSwitchTests` (the 2b world + a sibling over the emulated mirror + vault; ~73 s; no new opt-in env — the brief's "a new key-door drill env" reading was dropped because the doors' drills are cheap enough to run always), and the delete-and-set-up-again road is the standing opt-in pair (`PostureSwitchDrillTests` + `ConnectDrillTests`) re-run at the gate over the DEV-class scratch store (decision 26). The gate's legs per LIVE STATUS 3b.8.

#### ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES (settled under the mandate without the author — for the cold reviewer)
- The restored-copy enroll CORRECTION (ledger 52, at 3a's adjudication): the silent Standard enroll fires for ANY library with no custody material in hand whose recorded corpus id is nil or the vault's; a stale recorded id owes the follow for any device; the derived-key fill lands custody under the current shape for any gate — this SUPERSEDES 3a's THE SETTLED DESIGN item 2 ("never enrolled").
- The runner's nil-words guard (ledger 44): `run(entersCastle:…)` refuses `recoveryWords: nil` when the vault carries a recovery wrap — decision 33 at the RUNNER, not only the doors.
- The reset door's MERGE into change passphrase's forgot road (§13; the brain reshaped: Touch ID / the plain confirm authorizes `rewrapPassphrase` with the HELD material; the key file re-wrapped under the vault's new descriptor).
- The forget door's FOLD into Sign out (§13; decision 25): sign-out keeps custody; no user door clears it.
- Deletion by the ACCOUNT password with a fresh re-authentication; the encryption-passphrase authorization RETIRED from `AccountDeletion` (keystone 3); the fresh token pair discarded + revoked (the implementer's call within the brief's latitude).
- The export's local-table set read from `SyncSchema.localOnlyTables` (the registry), never a hand list.
- The two server auth settings (`password_min_length` 12, `password_hibp_enabled` on) are the orchestrator's PAT act at this batch's gate (ledger 45); the client renders the server's refusals; no client-side HIBP check.
- The Replace All Keys ceremony held on the controller (the Account sheet dismissed at the door) so the re-key's reopen never tears it down — 2b's precedent, applied by the implementer.

#### DRILLS RUN

**The cross-device drills (item 8; `App/Variorum/DerivedData/3b-drills-mac.log` — `✔ Test run with 23 tests in 1 suite passed after 72.732 seconds`; all three green on their first run):**
- **Change passphrase across two devices** — `PostureSwitchTests.changingThePassphraseOnOneDeviceRetiresTheOldWordAtTheVaultForItsSibling`: the 2b acting world + a sibling on the same mirror/vault/account; the acting device's `KeyDoorModel(.changePassphrase)` over PRODUCTION wiring (`wireOneSecretHooks` on the 2b controller) — a wrong current word refused against the vault, the change lands (the vault's revision moves); the sibling's `enroll` with the NEW word opens the SAME master and corpus id (a re-wrap, never a re-key), the OLD word refuses AT THE VAULT; the sibling's custody, library and rounds untouched — a pull + push pass with no fence, every acting title read in the clear; the acting key file's copy is under the NEW word with the VAULT's descriptor (`passphrase.kdf == vaultKDF == vault.format.kdf`). **RECORDED for batch 4:** the sibling's own key file keeps its copy under the OLD word (asserted) — its lobby still opens by the old word offline until batch 4's vault road updates it.
- **Regenerate across two devices** — `regeneratingTheWordsOnOneDeviceRetiresTheOldWordsAtTheVaultForItsSibling`: the vault's wrap under the old words first; the acting device's `KeyDoorModel(.regenerateWords)` authorizes by the passphrase, shows twelve fresh words once, lands them on confirm; the sibling's `enroll(recoveryWords:)` with the FRESH words opens the master, the OLD words refuse; the passphrase wrap untouched (independent wraps); no re-key, so the sibling has nothing to follow (`previousCorpusKey == nil`); the acting key file's W copy followed the same words.
- **Replace All Keys through the DOOR + the sibling's follow + a stale copy refused** — `replaceAllKeysThroughTheDoorIsFollowedByTheSiblingAndAStaleCopyOpensNothing`: the acting device walks `PostureSwitchModel(.replaceKeys)` — explain → type the words → authorize by the passphrase → done, no checkbox; custody re-saved silent (`.auto`), the posture Standard, the library under L′ with the Apple copy; **the stale copy** (the acting key file + custody as a backup made before the act holds them): its L through A opens no page of the re-keyed library, and its sync keys are refused at the mirror (`SyncError.encryptionKeysReplaced` on a pull); the sibling — that stale-custody device until it follows — is fenced with a pending row, `follow(passphrase:)` under Standard: no posture change, custody `.auto`, the vault's previous key + seq cleared, its library re-keyed under L′ (the old L opens nothing; the Apple copy again under its own A; L′ under M′), then a pull + push — the outbox empty, every acting title in the clear, and the pending row read back on the acting device.
- **Sign out everywhere against the fake auth** — `AuthSessionTests.signOutEverywhereRidesTheGlobalScopeAndClearsLocallyLikeTheOrdinarySignOut` (`3b-mac-doors.log`; red-on-old under batch D's D1): the logout URL carries `scope=global`, the bearer is the session's, the store clears; the ordinary door rides `scope=local`.
- **The export's strip** — core `StorePlaintextExportTests` (`3b-core-doors.log`; red-on-old under batch D's F1).
- **Delete and set up again (decision 26 — the DEV-class scratch store against the emulated mirror, never her account):** the standing opt-in pair re-run under `TEST_RUNNER_VARIORUM_POSTURE_DRILL=1 TEST_RUNNER_VARIORUM_CONNECT_DRILL=1` — `PostureSwitchDrillTests` (Standard → Castle over the sample library with a sibling, Castle → Standard, the three seams) + `ConnectDrillTests` (3a's adopt drill: delete → recreate → Connect over the sample library) — `✔ Test run with 3 tests in 2 suites passed after 91.405 seconds`, EXIT 0 (`3b-optin-drills.log`); real Keychain items only under the suites' throwaway scope.

- **The harness blocks a Bash command that BEGINS with `sleep`** (a sleep-prefixed command was refused); polling loops with `sleep 5` inside are fine. Long runs (the opt-in drills, the phone bundle, the Mac gate) were launched with `nohup … ; echo EXIT >> log` and polled in bounded foreground loops — never left to a background notification.

#### QUESTIONS FOR THE AUTHOR / ARCHITECT
1. **The descriptor floor vs. the test corpus — a MECHANISM reading, mine, recorded before the build:** the design's floor (m ≥ 19 MiB, t ≥ 2, 16-byte salt) applied unconditionally at the two read sites would refuse every fast test descriptor on the tree (31 sites at m = 256 KiB, t = 1 across the core, Mac and phone suites — the 13p-era "fast KDF" convention that keeps the suites' Argon2 cost near zero). Two honest shapes: **(A)** raise every test descriptor to the floor (~0.15 s per derivation on the M3; the gate slows by an estimated one to two minutes across the three targets, forever); **(B)** the floor the read sites consult is `.production` in every Release build and every non-test process, and relaxes ONLY in a test host, detected exactly as the shipped `KeychainTestTripwire.armed` does (`#if DEBUG` + the XCTest environment) — with the pin proving the production floor at both sites by injection. Built on (B) — the tripwire's own precedent, zero cost, the Release build compiled without the relaxation branch — as the PROVISIONAL; the architect's call, and (A) is a mechanical sweep if ruled.

a fresh local P copy under the vault's descriptor + `recordVaultReconciliation(kdf: the vault's, corpusKeyID: the vault's, recoveryWrapDigest: the vault's)` written only AFTER the vault unwrap AND the master-copy unwrap succeeded (a typo writes nothing); the words road the same way (`unwrapMaster(withRecoveryKey:)` → L; the W copy + the digest landed); the derived key of the SUCCESSFUL road held for the session (`holdDerivedWrappingKey` — the vault's descriptor when the vault opened, so 3b's rule (iii) lands custody at ready with no second entry); #### DRILLS RUN
- **(ii) THE REGENERATED WORDS ACROSS TWO DEVICES → THE SIBLING'S BANNER + DOOR** — inside `PostureSwitchTests.regeneratingTheWordsOnOneDeviceRetiresTheOldWordsAtTheVaultForItsSibling` (`4a-items234-mac.log` / `4a-items234-mac2.log`, green): the sibling's key file reconciled under the vault as it stood (the OLD wrap's digest); the acting device regenerates through the production `KeyDoorModel`; the acting key file records the NEW digest through the phrase-landed hook (it owes itself no notice); the sibling's reconcile ⇒ `.wordsRegeneratedElsewhere` (never a follow — `followPending` false); *Enter It Now…* → the words field; the OLD words refused (*Those aren't your account's twelve words.*); the NEW words land the sibling's W copy (it unwraps under them) with the vault's digest; the notice clears; nothing re-keyed; the next reconcile owes nothing.
- **(iii) THE UN-FOLLOWED DEVICE OPENED BY THE PASSPHRASE FOLLOWS WITH NO SECOND ENTRY** — `PostureSwitchTests.theFollowRunsFromTheWordJustTypedAtTheLobbyAndAsksWithoutAHeldKeyOrUnderAStaleOne` (the 2b world + two siblings; green): the acting device enters Castle; sibling one's session holds the lobby's derived key under the VAULT's descriptor; at ready `.movedToCastle` is owed; *Enter Passphrase…* (`choosePassphrase()`) asks NOTHING — the follow runs from the held key: M′ in custody, the library re-keyed once, the new key file's P copy under the vault's descriptor opening under the typed word, no W copy, no Apple copy; sibling two — a Touch ID open (no held key) is asked; a stale held key (another descriptor) is asked; the vault's descriptor with the WRONG word — the vault refuses ⇒ the field, nothing re-keyed, custody unchanged, no marker.
- **(iv) THE PHONE'S LOBBY VAULT ROAD ON THE SIMULATOR** — `MobileLobbyVaultRoadTests.theNewWordOpensThePhoneOnlineAndReWrapsItsCopyWhileTheOldWordOpensOffline` (quoted under 4a.1's LIVE STATUS line; `4a-item1-phone3.log` 11/3).
- **(i) THE PASSPHRASE CHANGE ACROSS TWO DEVICES, EXTENDED TO THE SIBLING'S LOBBY** (3b's recorded gap) — `PostureSwitchTests.changingThePassphraseOnOneDeviceRetiresTheOldWordAtTheVaultForItsSibling`, the RECORDED block replaced by the sibling's lobby over the real road, the 2b world's emulated vault (`4a-drill-i-mac.log`: `✔ Test run with 30 tests in 2 suites passed after 72.823 seconds` — PostureSwitchTests 23 + LobbyVaultRoadTests 7): the acting device changes the word through the production `KeyDoorModel`; the sibling's key file still under the OLD word; then at the sibling's lobby — (1) the OLD word OFFLINE opens by the local copy (1 derivation); (2) the NEW word ONLINE opens through the vault road (2 derivations — the sibling's wrap descriptor is its own `fastKDF()`, the vault's is the setUp's; the descriptors differ), the sibling's local copy RE-WRAPPED under the vault's descriptor (`passphrase.kdf == vault.format.kdf && vaultKDF == vault.format.kdf`; the new word unwraps, the old refuses), the held key the vault's derivation; (3) the NEW word OFFLINE opens by the local copy now (1 derivation); (4) the OLD word refused everywhere with the plain sentence. **The derivation count is TWO at the first online open here, not one:** the "one when the descriptors agree" case is the reconciled steady state (pinned in `LobbyVaultRoadTests` — a typo on a reconciled file derives once); a sibling whose local copy was written under its own descriptor derives once for the local attempt and once for the vault's — the second is what the road exists for.

#### MACHINERY NOTES
- The batch's DerivedData: `App/Variorum/DerivedData/pre13x-b0` (the Mac, reused), `pre13x-b0-ios` (the simulator), `pre13x-b0-ios-build` (the generic iOS build); logs `4a-*.log`, the scripts `4a-gate.sh` / `4a-sub.sh` / `4a-phone*.sh`, the pre-revert copies `4a-prerevert/` (gitignored; `git check-ignore` confirmed at the first commit).
- **A chained `cd` relative to a directory already entered SILENTLY killed two background legs** (the core run and the phone bundle "started" into a non-existent path — the log stayed 0 bytes): a background leg's log must be seen to GROW before it is trusted; relaunch from an absolute path. The core run's first launch died this way; the second (absolute) ran.
- `-only-testing` takes TYPE names (re-met, not tripped: the gate's 50 names ran as 50 suites); `test-without-building` runs the BUILT bundle and is immune to source edits — used for every run whose sources had not changed; a revert or a test edit needs `build-for-testing` first.
- The region-based isolation checker rejects a `withDeps { … }` body on a non-`@MainActor` suite ("pattern that the region based isolation checker does not understand") — the phone suites in `MobileProtectionTests` are `@MainActor` structs; the new phone suite is too. A local `func` captured by a `@Sendable` closure (`readVaultPosture`) must become a `@Sendable` closure constant; `#expect`'s autoclosure takes no `try` inside `&&` (bind first) — all three met.
- `ScriptedEnvelopeBox` vs the disk: the controller's per-store sync custody resolves the store id from the key file BESIDE THE STORE (`StoreIdentity.resolve`), so a bootstrap world whose envelope lives only in the box reads NO store id ⇒ no leaves ⇒ the NO-MATERIAL branch (the Later pin's part (2) owed nothing until the envelope was written to disk too); `Phase13pControllerTests.world(standard: false, enrolled: true)` needs a Castle-shaped `envelope:` passed in (P + master + the record).
- `AppBootstrap.lock()` used to drop `SyncCustodySession.shared` by name; it now drops the CONTROLLER's session (production's `.shared` — identical; a pin's injected session is dropped too). A behaviour precision, not a fix; no pin changed by it.
- A sibling world (`makeWorld(sharing:)`) seeds and pushes under the old keys — it must be built BEFORE the acting device's switch, or the fence refuses the seed (`SyncError.encryptionKeysReplaced`, met once).

1. **Derive ONCE on the WRAP's descriptor, not the recorded one** (ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES 2): the reuse condition is `envelope.passphrase.kdf == vault.kdf` — equal to the brief's "recorded `vaultKDF` equals the vault's" in every reconciled state and different only for the migration's leg-5 class, where reusing the key would be wrong. Pinned (a typo on the leg-5 class derives twice; on a reconciled file once). Confirm.

#### ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES (settled under the mandate without the author — for the cold reviewer; the readings this implementer built to)
1. **The vault road's timing (the brief's item 1):** the vault read starts concurrently with the local derivation on submit; the local verdict first; the vault awaited only on a local refusal; ~200 ms before the phase line; a ~10 s ceiling hands off to the local verdict. **The three sentences' conditions (the orchestrator's reading):** *That passphrase doesn't unlock this library.* — the local copy refused and the vault road refused or was unavailable while the recorded `vaultKDF` equals the vault's (or is nil / no vault answered); *Your passphrase was changed on another device — enter the new one.* — the local copy refused AND the vault road refused AND the recorded descriptor DIFFERS from the vault's; the offline note — after a submit in this lobby whose vault read failed with a transport error (never before a submit); the retired banner after an OLD word opened LOCALLY is item 2's ready-time notice.
2. **Derive ONCE — the mechanism's condition, precised by the implementer (recorded, not a fork):** the brief says the derived key is reused "when the key file's recorded `vaultKDF` equals the vault's". The derived key was made under the LOCAL WRAP's descriptor (`envelope.passphrase.kdf`), so the reuse that is mechanically correct is `wrap.kdf == vault.kdf` — identical to the brief's condition in every reconciled state (`reconcilePassphraseWrap` / `landVaultMaterial` / `writeNewKeyFile` land the P copy AND the record together) and different only for the migration's leg-5 class (the record set with the P copy still under the 13p descriptor — the confirm's class), where reusing the key would be wrong. The pin counts derivations on the wrap's condition.
3. **The words digest (ledger 53 — the orchestrator's MECHANISM ruling within §7's letter):** `vaultRecoveryWrapDigest` = SHA-256 hex of the vault's `masterWrappedByRecovery` bytes; recorded at EVERY reconciliation landing; nil reads as unknown and owes no notice; the acting device's own regenerate records the new digest through the phrase-landed hook so it never owes itself the notice.
4. **The held-key follow (ledger 54):** the follow runs from the session's derived key when its descriptor equals the vault's; the door's LABEL stays copy §5's *Enter Passphrase…* (13s's reading); a differing descriptor or no held key asks as today.
5. **`Later` per open (the 2c adjudication's item 1):** a relock + unlock is an open — the lock road resets the deferrals (the follow's, the migration confirm's, and — the same class — the change notice's dismissal).
6. **Held for the session — the reading:** the brief's "the material … is HELD for the session (`SyncCustodySession.hold`)" is built as the DERIVED KEY under the vault's descriptor (`holdDerivedWrappingKey`), because holding the RECORD would put the reconcile on its material-in-hand branch, which lands no custody — 3b's rule (iii) (the derived key opens the vault's master copy and `landEnrollment` lands custody under the current shape) is the pinned road, and the reconcile's own vault read is the one read at ready.

(9) NO ownership-change instruction exists on the wire: the client's RPC name set (read from the source files), the deployed function set (read from the SQL files 05 · 09 · 10 · 11) and the three transport protocols' requirements are asserted BY NAME — none moves rows between owners, none takes an owner parameter (the push/pull stamp `auth.uid()`); the only road between partitions is arm 8's local re-stamp + a push under the other account's own gate. And the post-switch pin: A's rows written after the switch open under NO key the attacker holds — the old M does not unwrap the vault's new class keys, the old C opens none of the server's rows (every row re-encrypted under C′), A opens no vault copy, and the bare session cannot pull them.
2. **Step 7's "the retry IS the follow" — the mechanism, precised at the runner:** the shipped `run()` left a `.vaultWrite` marker standing when `CorpusRotation.begin` met the vault's CAS refusal, and the follow only came at the marker's RESUME door (which re-reads the vault and asks the passphrase again). Corner 39's letter — "a CAS refuse at a posture switch re-reads: if the account already changed posture, the retry is the follow *(precised 2026-09-06 at 4b's adjudication — ledger 62: the refusal is the PROOF's when a rotation landed first, the CAS's when a re-wrap did; the runner re-reads on either)*" — is built IN PLACE: `run()` catches `KeyVaultClientError.vaultRevisionConflict` at the begin, re-fetches the vault; if the vault's corpus key id is no longer the one this device's custody holds (the account moved under it), the `.vaultWrite` marker is cleared and the road becomes the follow with the secret ALREADY TYPED for the ceremony (`VaultEnrollment.enroll(passphrase:)` → `runFollow` — no second rotation, no vault write of its own, the follow's own marker); if the key id did NOT move (a concurrent RE-WRAP — a passphrase change elsewhere — took the revision), the refusal is rethrown as before and the marker's resume road restarts the ceremony (its `restartRequired` arm), where the passphrase is re-verified against the vault's now-current copy (decision 10 — a retired word authorizes no key act). The ceremony's face shows its ordinary Done; a sentence naming "another device entered Castle first" is 13s's if wanted (QUESTIONS).
3. **Step 5's "the second re-reads and retries on top" at the change door:** the door reads the CAS refusal (`EncryptionSession.message(for: .vaultRevisionConflict)` — *The vault just changed on another device — nothing was overwritten. Try again from the current state.*) with nothing written, and RE-READS the vault's descriptor on that refusal so its next attempt classifies the typed word honestly: the OLD word (now retired at the vault) is worded RETIRED (copy §5), and the retry that lands on top is the FORGOT road from this device's held material (Touch ID / Face ID under Castle) or the twelve words — the roads that need no current word. The retry is the user's next press (the door never loops on its own); "the last change wins": the vault's copy is under the retrying device's word, the other device learns at its next open (the change notice; the vault road at its lobby).
4. **The un-followed device's "every key door speaks the sentence" — belt AND braces:** the faces already gate (the Protection pane on `followPending`; the Encryption tab on the session's `.keysReplaced` state — custody's class keys ≠ the vault's), and the MODELS now refuse too: `KeyDoorModel.start()` under `.keysReplaced` and `PostureSwitchModel.start()` under `followPending` (every mode but `.resume` — the marker's road IS a follow) answer `.failed(ProtectionCopy.pickUpKeysFirst)` before any field — so a composition that reaches a model without its face still speaks the design's one sentence. The `.castleStillOpensOldWay` kind (keys unchanged) gates the posture doors only (custody matches the vault, so the tab's re-wrap doors stay open — a re-wrap there is correct and the follow re-keys under the new word after).
5. **The core attacker world's "A enters Castle" is the runner's core road, not the runner:** `PostureSwitchRunner` is app-layer and invisible to the core suite; §4 steps 2–3 are exactly `CorpusRotation.begin(appleHeldCopy: nil)` → `reencryptRemainder` → `complete` (what the runner calls), step 4 is `StoreEncryptionMigration.rekey` + a new key file without an `apple` copy (what `writeNewKeyFile` + `swapLibrary` do), composed in the test's switch helper; the runner's own composition of that road is pinned in `PostureSwitchTests.expectCastleEndState`. The helper's "no Apple-held copy" decision is the scenario's fourth revertible gate (the brief's list).
6. **Both change at once / both switch at once are DETERMINISTIC in the pins:** the emulated vault's one-shot `beforeUpdate` hook (test support) runs the other device's whole act inside the first device's update, so the CAS refusal is produced by construction, never by a race the scheduler may or may not lose.

#### THE SETTLED DESIGN, PER ITEM
*(the LIVE STATUS lines above carry the built shape per item with their pins; this section holds what a reader needs beyond them)*
1. **The attacker scenario's world (core):** ONE `FakeSyncServer` (A = the default partition, B provisioned) LINKED to ONE `ScriptedVaultServer` (the `key_vault_update` twin over the same vault row); A's vault BORN Standard by the 3a road; A's library a keyed store ON DISK under `SyncClient(storeURL:custody:)` with a format-2 key file (P · W · M · A copies + the reconciliation); the pair through `AppDatabase.backup(to:)` (keyed under L — SQLCipher refuses an unkeyed backup) + the key file copied beside it; the attacker gathers M and C from the vault's Apple-held copy while Standard stands. `enterCastle()` = §4 steps 1–4 as the runner composes them (pull to head · `CorpusRotation.begin(appleHeldCopy: nil)` · the walk · `complete` · the pool CLOSED (`asRebindable().binding.lock()`) · `StoreEncryptionMigration.rekey` · a NEW key file under the new fingerprint with no `apple` · the device rebuilt as a fresh `SyncClient` over the re-keyed file with the SAME device identity — the bootstrap's reopen). The arms' order in the pin: 1 · 2 · 3 · 4 · 5 · 6 · the post-switch unreadability · 8 · 7 (deletion last, since A's partition must be byte-identical through the upload under B). Arm 9 is a text pin over the sources and the SQL chain (05 … 11).
2. **The choreography's deterministic races:** the `EmulatedVault` gains `setBeforeUpdate(_:)` — a ONE-SHOT hook run inside the next `key_vault_update` request before its CAS, consumed before it runs (its own writes pass through) — and `updateCount` (attempts, refused ones included). Step 5 runs device 1's core `changePassphrase` + a hand landing of its key file inside device 2's door write; step 7 runs device B's WHOLE `enterCastle` (a `@MainActor` task awaited from the hook) inside device A's rotation write.
3. **M1 — the runner's re-read (corner 39 / ledger 40):** in `run()`, `CorpusRotation.begin`'s refusal — `vaultRevisionConflict` OR `keyProofRefused` — re-fetches the vault; the corpus key id moved ⇒ `VaultEnrollment.enroll(passphrase: the ceremony's)` → the `.vaultWrite` marker cleared → `runFollow` (the follow's own markers, `follow: true`; the rotation road when custody's id differs; `complete` idempotent when the other device finished) with `KeyFileInputs(passphrase:, kdf: the vault's, recoveryWords: nil)` — the ceremony's words are NOT landed; the id unmoved ⇒ the refusal rethrown (the marker's resume road → `restartRequired` → the ceremony restarts with the word re-verified).
4. **M2 — the model guards:** `KeyDoorModel.start()`: `if case .keysReplaced = encryption.state { .failed(pickUpKeysFirst) }` before the offline fetch; `PostureSwitchModel.start()`: `if controller.followPending, !isResume { .failed(pickUpKeysFirst) }` before `checkDoor`.
5. **M3 — the door's re-read:** `KeyDoorModel.changePassphrase`, on the session's refusal equal to `EncryptionSession.message(for: .vaultRevisionConflict)`, re-fetches `vaultKDF` through `encryption.fetchVaultFacts()` so `wordRetiredIfStale` classifies the retry honestly.
6. **The un-followed pin's composition:** `controller(for:material:session:)` (the plain custody record — nil material = Always ask; the session; the runner; `foreignOwner` "mine"; the vault's reading over the shared client) + `wireSession(for:controller:)` (production `wireOneSecretHooks`) + `encryption.followDoor = { controller.openFollow() }` (the roots' line); the stale-proof engine over a `FixedKeyProvider` presenting the CURRENT key id with the OLD master's proof; `SyncRowFace.face(lastError: String(describing: error))` — the 2c pin's shape.
7. **The offline pin's composition:** a `KeyVaultClient` over a `load` that throws `URLError` while a `LockIsolated<Bool>` flag is set, else forwards to the emulated vault — so the session refreshes ONLINE first (`.enrolled`) and the doors then meet the network down at their threshold; the runner over `leaves(for: world, vaultOverride: flaky)`.

Its `✘` lines are the known-issue pair's four log lines (two `recorded a known issue`, the suite line, the run line) — count `Test run with … passed`, never `✘`.
- **`#expect`'s autoclosure takes no `await` and no `try` inside `&&`, and `LockIsolated.setValue`'s autoclosure takes no `await`:** bind first (met six times). A `@Sendable` hook reaching `@MainActor` state does it through `try await Task { @MainActor in … }.value` (a global-actor-isolated class is implicitly Sendable — capturing `World` is fine).
- **`StoreBinding.lock()` closes the SHARED writer:** `client.db.asRebindable().binding.lock()` closes the `SyncClient`'s pool too, so the post-switch device is a NEW `SyncClient` over the re-keyed file with the old device identity — the bootstrap's reopen. Two `DatabasePool`s on one WAL file (the re-setup client beside the post-switch client; the attacker's client beside the snapshot view) are fine.
- **A backup's outbox is EMPTY when the source had synced:** arm 8's refused push needs `resyncReinitialize` FIRST (every row enqueued, still stamped A) — a plain push carried only the attacker's own note. The stale device opens its OWN copy of the backup (an attacker copies freely) so its typed row never rides into B's count.
- **The `send("sync_schema_version")` road is not an `rpc(…)` call** — the wire pin's regex reads both (`(?:rpc|send)\(\s*"…"`).
- **The emulation's RPC order is the SQL's — proof BEFORE CAS:** the second of two concurrent SWITCHES meets `VARIORUM_KEY_PROOF_REQUIRED` (the first rotation replaced the hash), not the revision conflict; the runner's re-read catches both (QUESTIONS 1).
- The `EmulatedVault`'s hook runs with NO lock held, so the other device's whole act — its own vault reads and writes through the same emulated vault and the same `KeyVaultClient` actor (suspended at the first device's `load` await) — is re-entrant by construction.

#### QUESTIONS FOR THE AUTHOR / ARCHITECT
*(none gated the build; each is a READING settled under the mandate and recorded for the orchestrator's adjudication)*
1. **Corner 39's "CAS refuse" is the PROOF's refusal when a rotation landed first** — `key_vault_update` checks the proof before the revision (11's order, the fakes' too), and the first switch replaced the hash — and the CAS's only when a RE-WRAP intervened. The runner re-reads on either (`vaultRevisionConflict` or `keyProofRefused` at `begin`) and follows when the corpus key id moved. Confirm the reading; the design's letter names the CAS alone.
2. **The conflict-follow's twelve words (a product corner the design does not cover):** two devices entering Castle at once each GENERATE fresh words (or type them); the loser's ceremony has shown its user twelve words that never landed — the vault's words are the winner's. The follow lands no W copy (the ceremony's words are not the account's) and the ceremony's Done says nothing of it. Her call: a sentence at the conflict-follow's Done (*"another device entered Castle first — the twelve words shown here are not your account's; use the ones written down on [device]"*, 13s), or a `PostureSwitchRunner.Outcome` flag the face reads. Recorded, not built.
3. **Step 5's "retries on top" at the door is the USER's next press** (the door never loops): the door re-reads the vault's descriptor on the conflict refusal (M3) so the retry with the now-retired word is worded RETIRED, and the road that lands on top is the forgot road (the held material, Touch ID / Face ID under Castle) or the twelve words. Confirm; an automatic retry was deliberately not built (a retry that re-authorizes by a retired word would refuse anyway, and one that retries with the held material silently would install a passphrase the user is not looking at).
4. **The model-level door guards (M2)** duplicate the faces' gates (the pane on `followPending`, the tab on `.keysReplaced`) so the models refuse on their own; `.castleStillOpensOldWay` (keys unchanged) gates the POSTURE doors only — the tab's re-wrap doors stay open because custody matches the vault and a re-wrap there is correct (the follow then re-keys under the new word). Confirm.
5. **The core attacker scenario composes the runner's core road** (the runner is app-layer); arm 7's "sign out everywhere" is cited from 3b's `AuthSessionTests` (no auth fake in core). Confirm.
6. **The choreography's Always-ask device** is modelled by custody NOWHERE + the session's hold (the 2b world's dials box is not reachable from a sibling, so the gate's `alwaysAsk` dial is not set); the reconcile's rule (b) fires for any gate and the kind is `.alwaysAskRetiredWord`. Recorded.
7. **The offline sentences** (13s): regenerate speaks change-passphrase's `changePassphraseOffline`; Replace All Keys and both switches speak the runner's URLError wording — copy §2b/§4 carry no offline line for them.
8. **Ledger 40 was built here, not only pinned** (the brief listed it under 4b as "a CAS refuse at a switch becomes the follow"; the shipped runner left a `.vaultWrite` marker and the follow came only at the resume door, asking the passphrase again) — the orchestrator's flip.

#### ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES (settled under the mandate without the author — for the cold reviewer; the readings this implementer builds to)
1. **The iCloud Drive offer (item 0(a) — ledger 64):** ONE sheet, ONCE per store, at the FIRST backup run, over the 13l `BackupDestination` picked-folder machinery; a defaults mark per store variant on the `SampleLibrarySeeded.<variant>` shape (a dev-store offer never suppresses the real store's); *Use iCloud Drive* points the second destination at the user's iCloud Drive; *Choose Another Folder…* the existing picker; *Not Now* marks it asked; the Mac only (the phone has no picked-folder road); the picker's live-library refusal stays 13x's. **The implementer's readings inside that ruling (recorded, to be adjudicated):** (i) the app is SANDBOXED (the Variorum entitlements file merges with the target's `ENABLE_APP_SANDBOX` settings — `BackupDestination`'s own header says so), so no code path may write into `~/Library/Mobile Documents/com~apple~CloudDocs` on its own: *Use iCloud Drive* opens the folder picker ALREADY POINTED at iCloud Drive's root (`NSOpenPanel.directoryURL` — the powerbox honors the hint; a user-selected folder is the only sandbox-legal road to a folder outside the container), and the pick lands the backups at `<iCloud Drive>/Variorum/Backups` — the 13l folder shape inside the pick, in place of the brief's literal `…/Variorum Backups` name (the brief's path is the intent, the 13l shape is the built machinery). (ii) A cancelled picker (iCloud Drive missing, or the user backing out) leaves the sheet OPEN with a short note and the mark UNSET — *Not Now* is the door that marks it asked; the copy's why-not sentence (the live library in iCloud Drive) states a fact about the LIBRARY, not about a missing iCloud Drive, so it is NOT rendered for that case — the note is a PROVISIONAL chrome line listed in copy §11 (QUESTIONS). (iii) A store that ALREADY has a second folder picked when its first run lands is marked asked silently — the question is answered by the pick that exists. (iv) The mark is read and written per store VARIANT (`variorum.backup.iCloudDriveOffered.<variant>`).
2. **The un-followed headline (item 0(b) — ledger 66):** copy §4's precised sentence as `ProtectionCopy.keysReplacedHeadline` (the device word by platform); `EncryptionView.keysReplacedExplanation` becomes that constant (the shared view serves both platforms — the phone's "twin" is the same view in its nouns); the no-controller arm's caption and the ordinary re-enroll roads unchanged; the Standard addendum rendered nowhere.
3. **The resume road's flag (item 0(c) — ledger 67):** at `.vaultWrite` with the vault's corpus key id moved, the runner cannot tell "my write landed, custody did not" from "another device's rotation landed instead" by key material (a never-landed write left nothing behind; `CorpusRotation.begin` mints inside `Sync/`, untouchable this batch). The discriminator is the vault's self-reported `updated_by_device` (decision 34; ledger 49's disclosure applies) against this device's own name (`Leaves.deviceName`, production `DeviceName.current` — the same name the vault client stamps): another device's name ⇒ the FOLLOW with the flag, the ceremony's words NOT landed (M1's rule); this device's name ⇒ the existing road (its own write landed). **The recorded corner:** a write of this device's that landed, then a FOLLOWER that finished the rotation (its clearing write stamps ITS name), then this device's resume — reads as another device's rotation and speaks the conflict sentence over words that WERE the account's: the false-alarm direction, chosen over the vacuous-promise direction (QUESTIONS).
4. **The lock-time copy line (item 0(d) — ledger 68):** rendered under Castle only (the copy's own placement); the phone has neither a lock-time copy (`performLockTimeCopyIfDue` is wired in `VariorumApp` alone) nor a Backups face — nothing owed there.
5. **The indicator (item 1):** ONE Shared view, `TravellingDotsIndicator` — a hairline with three dots travelling back and forth on `TimelineView(.animation)`; the ink is the caller's token (the voice's `muted`/`ink`, or the shared page style's ink; `.secondary` where a Mac page has no tokens — a semantic, never a literal); Reduce Motion ⇒ the dots rest evenly spaced; the accessibility label *Working…*. The SITES: the brief's list, plus the same family's siblings found by the walk — `SyncEnrollmentView` (the Turn On Sync ceremony), `SecretCheck` (the Protection pane's check), the Mac `LibraryMissingView` (the phone's twin is on the brief's list), `ProtectionPane`'s sheets (`PassphraseSetSheet` · `FollowSheet` · `MigrationConfirmSheet` · `PostureSwitchSheet`). The REST keeps `ProgressView` (the Backups tab, the Account tab's sign-in, the sync row, the book/reference/export surfaces).
6. **The runbook (item 2):** rewritten in its own voice per posture, facts from design §1–§7 only; the picker's live-library refusal and a remaining-rows count are NOT promised; the iCloud Drive offer and the lock-time copy ARE (once item 0 lands). §8 stays COMPOSED from the posture cards' floors (the 5a pin) and gains the roads.
7. **PL-20 (item 5):** the phone's INSIDE-the-phase deliverables listed; the ONE owed item is the plaintext-export door (no document-picker road on the phone — P-2); nothing else mobile-visible.

1. **Copy §8 names 13x's open-a-copy door three times** (*Moving to a new Mac*: "open a copy of your library from your backup folder" and "open a backup and type them"; *Restoring a backup*: "Open a copy through File ▸ Open Library") — a door the build does not have (ledger 5, 13x). STOPPED: the entries are not rewritten (hers / 13s's); a dated build-vs-copy note stands beside §8; the outbound FAQ brief marks each ⚠ NOT YET BUILT and names today's road (the runbook's `RESTORE.md` recipe — quit, move the library aside, put the copy in its place, open — which ends where the entries say: the copy opens by its key file's roads and enrolls). The orchestrator's call: publish those entries when 13x lands, or re-word them onto the recipe now.
2. **The iCloud Drive FAQ entry was DRAFTED into copy §8** (*Can my library live in iCloud Drive?* — §6's why-not sentence verbatim, as §6's own parenthetical names "the FAQ" as its home) — confirm, or move it.
3. **The resume road's discriminator (0(c)) is the vault's self-reported device name, and it has ONE corner:** a `.vaultWrite` marker with the vault's key id moved cannot be told "my write landed" from "another device rotated" by key material (a never-landed write leaves nothing; `CorpusRotation.begin` mints inside `Sync/`, untouchable at 5b). Built: `updated_by_device` ≠ this device's name ⇒ the follow with the flag and the words NOT landed. The corner: this device's write landed, custody failed, a FOLLOWER finished the rotation (its clearing write stamps its own name), then this device resumed — reads as another device's rotation and speaks the conflict sentence over words that WERE the account's (the false-alarm direction, chosen over the vacuous-promise one — the shape of ledger 67's own case is the common one: I crash, the other device runs its whole ceremony, I relaunch). Two same-named devices read as one (the degradation is to 5a's behaviour, never worse). A future exact discriminator is a marker field the vault write itself could stamp (`Sync/`, 18a-shaped). Confirm the reading, or rule the corner the other way.
4. **`ProtectionCopy.liveLibraryNotInICloudDrive` exists as a constant and renders nowhere in this phase** — copy §6 names it as the FAQ's and the folder picker's refusal's; the refusal is 13x's (ledger 4). One home for the sentence, ahead of its door; confirm, or delete the constant until 13x.
6. **The runbook's restore recipe is SCOPED to a copy under the library's CURRENT key** (the everyday case, the 13l drill's shape): a copy from before a Replace All Keys or entering Castle is under a key this Mac no longer holds, and on the SAME Mac the store id names a custody item holding the current key — what the gate does when custody's key does not open a restored older-key file is 13x's open-door territory (design §6's "second look", ledger 5), not a road the runbook may promise; the recipe says such a copy opens with its own key file on a Mac where this library does not live (§3.5's fingerprint rule + the lobby's roads — pinned at 3b.0 for the enroll half). Confirm the scope, or rule the same-Mac older-key road into 13x's brief explicitly. *(This entry was meant for the 5b.2 commit, whose message names it; its record edit missed its anchor and it landed here at the gate — recorded.)*
7. **OBSERVED on the DEV store at the walk (not 5b's to fix): *Encryption Explained* did not seed** — the shelf held five books after this build's launch. The seeder's gate (R-pre13u-1: an account-owned store seeds nothing — arrive-by-sync) reads the dev build's signed-in DEV-scoped session as "belongs to an account", while the dev variant's sync is OFF — so the dev store neither seeds the book nor receives it. 5a's expectation ("the Debug build seeds both books at its next launch") holds only for a signed-out dev store. For the orchestrator: sign the dev build out, or restore through Help ▸ Restore “Encryption Explained” (5a's door), before walking the book on the dev store.
8. **The DEV store is Castle with NO passphrase and NO words** (the Protection pane: Touch ID unlocks it; *Encryption passphrase — not set*; *Recovery phrase — not saved*; the exposed nudge speaking) — the state the orchestrator's walks left it in; 5b changed nothing (the wrong-word probe refused before any write). Recorded so the walk's reader does not take it for a 5b act.
5. **The iCloud Drive offer's shape under the sandbox** (ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES 1): *Use iCloud Drive* opens the folder PANEL at iCloud Drive's root rather than writing a folder there itself (the app is sandboxed; a user-selected folder is the only legal road out of the container); the backups land at `<pick>/Variorum/Backups` (the 13l shape) rather than the brief's literal `…/Variorum Backups` name; a cancelled panel leaves the sheet open with `iCloudOfferNoFolderPicked` and the mark unset (the why-not sentence is not rendered there — it states a fact about the library, not about a missing iCloud Drive).
