**MAJOR-3 REFINED (the orchestrator, 2026-09-06, on the author's question "is being able to rotate the iCloud key going to be an issue, or our lack of the ability to do that?" — the answer is decision 30 / V.1: A is deliberately never rotated (rotating A inside the same Apple account defends against nothing; entering Castle IS the rotation that removes A); what MAJOR-3 found is A being DUPLICATED by accident, and the reviewer's proposed (a)/(c) would have broken the different-Apple-ID road of corner 34 / ledger 48 — a device on a different Apple ID reads NO A and must mint its own for ITS file, while (c) as proposed would let it re-land the VAULT's copy under its own Apple account, flip-flopping the account's Apple key between two Apple IDs. The client cannot tell "same Apple ID, propagation lag" from "a different Apple ID" (no iCloud-account identity is stored; the ubiquity token needs an entitlement the app does not carry). The exact rules that need no such identity):** **(a) MINT only from a device that HOLDS M** — the account's first Standard device at sync setup, a device leaving Castle (the vault carries no Apple copy — whoever leaves Castle establishes A), or a device that ENROLLED by a typed secret and still reads no A afterwards: it mints A′ and lands ITS OWN FILE's copy under A′ — never the vault's copy (a different Apple ID's device keeps the account's vault under the first Apple ID's A, exactly corner 34's disclosure); a device that does NOT hold M never mints — it shows the waiting face (copy §3, its passphrase doors) while `read()` is nil. **(b) Replace All Keys under Standard uses `read()` only and REFUSES when A is unreadable** (the PROVISIONAL sentence; a different-Apple-ID device therefore cannot run it — correct: it could only hand M′ to the wrong Apple account's key). **(c) HEAL only on proof of a same-account duplicate:** iff this device's own key file's recorded `appleKeyID` EQUALS the vault's `apple_key_id` AND `read()` now returns a DIFFERENT id — this device's own iCloud Keychain replaced the key it minted, which only the same Apple account can do — then, holding M, it re-lands the vault's copy and its file copy under the readable A in one proof-authorized write; a device whose recorded id differs from the vault's never heals the vault (it re-lands only its own file copy under what it reads, as today). Convergence: whichever minted key iCloud keeps, the device that recorded the losing id sees its keychain changed under it and heals; the other re-lands its file. **(d) The `differentAppleID` label is HEDGED** — when `read()` returns an id ≠ the vault's, the face's sentence must cover both cases (copy §3's line re-worded PROVISIONAL: *This Mac's Apple account doesn't hold the key that set up sync — if it's the same Apple account, the key may still be arriving. Enter your passphrase or twelve words once to continue now; after that this Mac opens on its own.*). **Pins:** (a) a Standard vault naming `A₁`, `read()` nil, no M in hand ⇒ no mint (red today); a device holding M by a typed secret with `read()` nil ⇒ mints and lands ITS file copy, the vault's copy untouched; (b) Replace All Keys with A unreadable refuses (red today: it mints); (c) recorded == vault ≠ read ⇒ the heal; recorded ≠ vault ⇒ no vault write; (d) the copy pin.

##### THE TRACED MECHANISM (restated in my own words — the hallucination guard; traced BEFORE the fix)
- **BLOCKER-1.** `sync_pull` never serves a row whose `server_written_at` is within 2 s (11's lines 587/599). A push racing the switch read the vault row `FOR SHARE` under the OLD proof; `key_vault_update`'s `FOR UPDATE` waits for it, so its rows commit — and take seqs — BELOW `rotation_started_seq` (decision 31, pinned), yet stay invisible to every pull for ≤ 2 s. `CorpusRotation.reencryptRemainder` (374–384 on the pre-fix tree) pulled `since: 0` with `pullBelow`, re-sealed what it SAW, and returned at the first EMPTY window with no wait anywhere; on a small corpus both passes finish inside the window. `complete` (444–461) then wrote `clearingPreviousCorpusKey()` — the previous key, its id and the seq left the vault — and the runner's `reencryptAndComplete` (760–763) saved `material.clearingPreviousCorpusKey()` into custody — the previous key left custody. The straggler sat on the mirror below a seq nobody records, sealed under the OLD C with AAD `keyID = the old id`. Every later `SyncEngine.pull` (374) opened it through `keyMaterial(forServerSeq:)` — `keys.rotation == nil` ⇒ the CURRENT key + current id — so `PayloadCipher.open` failed (wrong key AND wrong AAD) and `pulled.append(contentsOf: try window.map(crypto.decryptFromWire))` threw `SyncError.payloadCryptoFailure`: the WHOLE pull refused, cursor unmoved, on every device (a follower's first pull at cursor 0 died the same way), forever; only a push CAS-updating that exact row could heal it. The existing pin `theWalkReencryptsEveryRowBelowTheSeqAndCompletionClearsTheKeyAndTheSeqTogether` passed only because its `SeamTransport(afterFirstReencrypt: .advanceClock(2))` let time pass between passes — production had no such clock. **Confirmed by running:** with the client wait and the fake's guard reverted (R1), the reviewer's pin reddens exactly there (quoted under PINS).
- **One precision on the orchestrator's qualification (recorded, not a disagreement):** the walk DOES hand all-plain rows to `sync_reencrypt` when it sees them (`pullBelow` returns every table; the reseal of a plain row is the identity, the re-stamp is real — 11's `sync_reencrypt` re-stamps every `allowed` table), so plain rows are not "never re-stamped". The exclusion is still exactly right, for the reason that matters: a plain row below the seq is under NO key (`decryptFromWire` returns a plain-table row untouched — `guard !table.encryptedColumns.isEmpty else { return row }`), so its visibility inside the window must never hold a completion — while counting it would make the guard depend on a row with no ciphertext at stake. Pinned as its own fence (`aStragglerInAnAllPlainTableNeverHoldsACompletion`).
- **Why ONE wait suffices (the client half's shape):** every row that can sit below the seq committed BEFORE the vault write (the FOR SHARE / FOR UPDATE ordering); the walk starts after that write; so once ≥ 2.5 s have passed after the walk's first empty window, every below-seq row is older than the horizon and visible to the pull that follows. A row found then is re-encrypted (re-stamped above the seq) and the next empty window ends the walk. The server guard is the structure; the wait is the insurance that makes the guard's refusal rare.
- **Ledger 69.** On the pre-F1 tree `CorpusRotation.begin` fetched, checked the door, MINTED (`UUID().uuidString` for the corpus id, inside the call) and wrote in one breath; the runner wrote its `.vaultWrite` marker before calling it with `newCorpusKeyID` nil (the id did not exist yet) and learned the id only from `begun.material` — after the write. So a resume at `.vaultWrite` with the vault's id moved had nothing of its own to compare: 5b read `updated_by_device ≠ this device's name` as "another device rotated". The recorded corner: our write landed (the vault's id is OURS), custody failed (the fault after the write), a FOLLOWER finished the rotation — its clearing write is a `key_vault_update` too, so `updated_by_device` became "sibling device" — and our resume then read the follower's name as another device's ROTATION: the conflict-follow sentence over words that WERE the account's, and the words not landed (`recoveryWords: byAnotherDevice ? nil : typedWords`). The fix is one seam: mint before the marker, stamp the id, compare ids. **Confirmed by running:** with the discriminator reverted to the name reading (R3), the corner pin reddens on exactly the flag and the un-landed words (quoted under PINS); the 5b pin holds under BOTH readings (B's name ≠ A's and B's id ≠ A's stamped id), which is why the corner pin, not the 5b pin, is what tells the readings apart.
- **The old client (her Mac's shipped build) converges on the guard — verified by READING the `.reencrypt` resume road:** its `reencryptAndComplete` calls `complete` ONCE; `updateVault` meets the new marker, `classifiedUpdateFailure` (pre-F1) knows no such marker ⇒ `.httpFailure(status:body:)` ⇒ thrown ⇒ `run` / `resume` throws with the marker STILL at `.reencrypt` (written before the walk) and custody holding M′ + the previous key; every engine round refuses (`accountRekeyIncomplete`); the resume door (*Finish Entering Castle…* → `resume(passphrase: nil)`) takes the `.reencrypt` arm — custody's material carries the rotation — and re-runs `reencryptRemainder` (the old walk, no wait) which by then FINDS the straggler (time has passed), then `complete` succeeds. The refusal it shows is the raw `httpFailure` sentence (ugly, honest, nothing lost); the resume converges. Deploy order safe both ways: the guard on the mirror with an old client (above); a new client against the old mirror (the client wait alone catches the straggler).

- `SyncSessionFenceTests.aStoppedSessionRunsNothingOnTriggerAndTheReconnectRoadRunsARoundAgain` and `AccountDoorWiringTests.theCeremoniesResumeLeafReconnectsAStoppedChainAndLeavesALiveOneAlone` are MECHANISM / COMPOSITION pins over the session and the production leaf — no behavioural revert exists for "a trigger after stop() is a no-op" (that IS the old tree's fact, asserted as the mechanism) and the leaf pin would red on the pre-F2 tree by compile (`AppServices.resumeSync()` did not exist).

##### THE TRACED MECHANISM (restated in my own words — the hallucination guard; traced BEFORE each fix)
- **MAJOR-1.** `runCeremony` called `leaves.pauseSync()` before its drain; both follows called it before the vault unwrap. The production leaf was `services()?.sync.stop()` — `SyncSession.stop()` sets `scheduler = nil` and retires the old chain. `leaves.resumeSync()` rode ONLY the success roads (`rekeyCustodyAndSnapshot`'s end, the resume's last arm, `pickUpKeys`) and its production leaf was `services()?.sync.triggerSync()` — `guard let scheduler else { return }`: a no-op on a stopped session. Success masked it: the re-key's reopen retires the old services (`onWillLock` → `services = nil`) and the fresh ones connect in their own `start()`. Every failure BEFORE the reopen — `outboxNotEmpty`, `pullStalled`, `rotationInProgress`, `wordsRequired`, `syncKeysUnavailable`, a vault refusal in `begin`, a wrong follow passphrase, and since F1 `completionRefused` — threw with the chain retired and no reopen: sync dead until relaunch, relock or sign-out/in, and the `outboxNotEmpty` sentence asked the user to wait for a sync that could not run. `deleteAccount` already had the right shape (`sync.stop()` … on refusal `await connectSyncIfAllowed()`). **Confirmed by running:** R1 (the catch without the resume) reddened `resumes → 0` at all four exits and F1's `completionRefused` exit.
- **Why the production leaf is reconnect-OR-trigger, not reconnect always:** on the success road `services()` is read LIVE — nil while the `.ready` tree's `.task` has not yet rebuilt the services (their own `start()` connects; nothing owed), or the fresh, already-connected services (a second `configure` would drain and replace a working chain — a bare trigger is what is owed). The one benign race: the fresh services assigned but `start()` not yet at its `connectSyncIfAllowed` when the leaf runs — two `configure`s, the second draining the first; `runOwnerAdoption` twice, idempotent. Recorded, not pinned (a timing of the root's `.task`).
- **MAJOR-2.** Step 6 was `_ = await leaves.takeSnapshot()` — the leaf answered `URL?` (nil = the run did not write, OR this platform keeps no backups — the phone's `AppServices.backup` is nil) and the runner discarded it; the pre-switch pairs were listed whenever `listPreSwitchPairs` and the Done rendered `enteredCastleDone` ("A fresh backup was made under it"). So an unwritable Backups folder, a WAL that would not drain, any `performRun` failure ⇒ the user was offered — with the sentence's assurance — the deletion of the only snapshots that existed; `Delete Them` removed every app-made pair. The same arm rode the follow and the resume's `.snapshot` step (`rekeyCustodyAndSnapshot` is shared). **Confirmed by running:** R2 reddened the listing (`[…/variorum-backup-d60a55d6-0.sqlite] == []`), the act (`deletePreSwitchSnapshots → 1`, the file gone) and the Done's claim.
- **MAJOR-3 (a).** `reconcileAppleKeyOffMain` ran at every ready under a DETERMINED Standard reading and minted A whenever `read()` was nil. A signed-in library on a NEW device of the same Apple ID whose A had not propagated (§3.8: "unbounded in practice") reached ready — the route's waiting state covers only the absent-bound-item case; a fresh library routes `.open` and mints its own L — and the arm minted A₂ and wrote it synchronizable: two items under one `(service, account, synchronizable, group)` identity, iCloud keeping one (batch 0's recorded fact: most-recent-version-wins). Whichever it kept, the VAULT's `master_wrapped_by_apple_held` stayed under A₁ (no road re-wrote it but sync setup, `leaveCastle` and a Standard Replace All Keys), so every later Apple device read `apple.id != vault.appleKeyID` ⇒ `differentAppleID` ⇒ a false sentence and Standard's nothing-typed enroll dead for the account. The client cannot tell "same Apple ID under lag" from "a different Apple ID" (no iCloud identity is stored) — the refined rule needs none: mint only holding M. **Confirmed by running:** R3 reddened `w.appleKeys.key → AppleKey(…) == nil` under a vault naming A₁ with no custody.
- **MAJOR-3 (c).** No road ever re-landed the vault's copy after iCloud resolved a duplicate. The proof of a SAME-account duplicate lives in this device's own key file: it recorded the vault's `apple_key_id` (it minted that key, or landed under it) and its own iCloud Keychain now hands back a different id — only the same Apple account can replace the key this device minted. The heal must write the vault FIRST: the old arm re-landed the file's copy under the readable A unconditionally, which would have erased the proof before any vault write and left the vault stranded forever. **Confirmed by running:** R3 reddened `healed.value → []` and the refused-write corner (`appleKeyID → "e6d9574e-…" == "a1-the-accounts"` — the file re-landed, the proof lost).
- **MAJOR-3 (b).** `appleHeldCopyForStandard()` used `readOrMint()`: a Standard Replace All Keys with A momentarily unreadable MINTED a new A and wrapped M′ under it — decision 30 says in so many words "never re-mints the iCloud key" — and every other device's key file and the previous vault copy then named an id iCloud might retire. And with A readable but ANOTHER Apple account's (corner 34's device), the copy of M′ would have gone under that account's key. The runner's guard now reads (never mints) and requires the id to be the vault's when the vault names one; the check keys on the vault's `appleKeyID` at step 2, after the drain — inside the balanced pause, so the refusal resumes the chain. **Confirmed by running:** R3 reddened `an error was expected but none was thrown` · `appleBacking.key → AppleKey(…) == nil` · the vault rotated, on both arms.
- **MAJOR-3 (d).** `waitingDifferentAppleID` asserted a different Apple account; the code that renders it (`StoreLobby.openWithAppleKey` → `.differentAppleID`; `EnrollFacts.AppleState.differentAppleID`) knows only that the id read is not the copy's — a same-account key still arriving reads the same. One constant, three renderers (the lobby on both platforms, the enroll face) — hedged once.
- **MINOR-4.** `DoorFacts` carried `vaultUpdatedAt` but not `updatedByDevice`, so both the door (`start()`) and the mid-run refusal (`message(for: .rotationInProgress)`) rendered "another device" — the one sentence decision 34 was ruled for. The vault's fetched format carries the name; the door check now hands it over and the model renders it at both sites. **Confirmed by running:** R4 reddened both sentences ("started on another device" where the vault names Lillian's iPhone).
- **MINOR-5.** The ceremony's drain built `SyncEngine(transport:keyProvider:)` bare; `sampleDedupeOccasion` guards on `!sampleLibraryFacts.isEmpty`, so a seeded-book `reading` row landing during the drain's pull to head recorded no occasion, and the regular engine never saw that arrival again (its cursor had moved). Narrow (the other device's seed must land during the seconds of the drain), self-healing at the next `reading` / reserved-tag arrival — but a second engine composition silently dropping a repair. The facts now ride the leaves; a composition pin (no revert: the mechanism is an absence).
- **MINOR-8.** `TutorialSeeder` seeds by its own rule; `SampleLibrarySeeder.seedIfNeeded` declines on a store that belongs to an account and has never synced cleanly (ledger 70's 13u-era gate, untouched) — so on exactly that store Lesson 2's last paragraph named a book absent from the shelf, on the Mac until Help ▸ Restore, on the phone with no door at all. The tutorial door now ensures the book by the restore road (mint what is missing; nothing of hers touched); the phone gains the door. **Confirmed by running:** R5 reddened `after.complete → false` after the door opened the tutorial. Recorded race, pre-existing for Help ▸ Restore too: a user opening the tutorial INSIDE the launch seed's 20-second first-round wait could seed beside it (both roads discover-then-mint; no shared latch) — two books, `discover` then reads the first by name; not built against here (the restore door carried the same window before F2).
- **NIT-3.** `.snapshot` / `.offer` markers are written at `.rekeyLibrary` with `newKeyFingerprint` and every `advanced(to:)` carries it — the `?? StoreKeyEnvelope.fingerprint(of: … ?? StoreKey.mint())` fallback was unreachable, and had it ever run it would have named the snapshot's pair by a key that never existed. **NIT-2** was a `let` computed and discarded. **NIT-5**: retention prunes (`prune(directory:keep:)` — 14 dailies + 4 weeklies); "never changes" read as "never removes" to a reader who finds an old backup gone.

##### MACHINERY NOTES
- **`git checkout --` after a `cd App/Variorum` misses a repo-relative path** — met THREE times this session (R1, R4, R5; F1 recorded it once). Every restore must run from the repo root, or with a path relative to the `cd`. The `cmp` against the pre-revert copy is what caught each miss; never announce "restored" before the `cmp`.
- `-only-testing:VariorumTests/<Name>` selects ZERO tests for a FILE name (`OwnerIdentityTests`, `MobileTutorialWalkTests`, `MobileProtectionTests`, `KeychainOffMainTests`, `Phase13pProtectionBrainTests` are files): TYPE names only — `grep "struct <Name>\b"` before listing.
- `#expect`'s autoclosure refuses a `try` to the right of `&&` — bind first (met once more, MAJOR-2's pin).
- `SampleLibrarySeeder.Existing.isComplete(for:)` is MainActor-isolated (the enum is `@MainActor`): return `Existing` from `database.read` and ask it on the actor; `Queries.ReadingEntry`'s post id is `postID`.
- The Swift memberwise init orders labels by declaration: a new `Outcome`/`Leaves` field with a default must be passed in declaration order at every explicit call site.
- A revert's own artifact can sit beside the mechanism's red (R1: `pauses → 1 == 2` because the reverted tree never cleared `syncPaused`) — quote the mechanism's line and name the artifact, never quote the count as the finding.
- The session's DerivedData: `pre13x-b0` (Mac), `pre13x-b0-ios` (the simulator), `pre13x-b0-ios-build` (the generic build); logs `F2-*.log`; the pre-revert copies `F2-prerevert/` (all gitignored). Incremental `build-for-testing` ~50 s on the Mac, ~30 s on the simulator; `PostureSwitchTests` ~145 s per run.
